# Theory of finite quantum mechanics: derivation tree

**AuthorityEffect:** None.

This file is a table of contents for `theory_of_finite_qm.jsonl`. Each row is a finite derivation step that Python, Rust, and Lean 4 still have to carry, or a source record those languages have to match. The graph is a tree rooted at the inert inscription `<>`. A `GAP` or `OPEN_OBLIGATION` status is unfinished work. A computational control is not a completed derivation. Statuses other than SOURCE_RECORD, OPEN_OBLIGATION, GAP, and COMPUTATIONAL_CONTROL are fences from the source slice, including a float script and an exact area-deficit proxy that is not the Einstein-Hilbert action. F002 remains an interface inventory. Continuum QED, continuum QCD, and continuum quantum gravity are not derived here.

Nodes: 430. Dropped atlas paths that were not on disk: 0.

## PREGENESIS

### TFQ-0000 Inert inscription <>

- UUID: `a03940f6-7133-54e7-a6b9-2326971e931d`
- Parent: `root`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`
- Coda atlas: `F000`, `F000.S000`
- Atlas paths:
  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F000_finite/floor.yaml`
  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F000_finite/S000_primitive_difference_and_finite_obstruction/shelf.yaml`

Pre-Genesis section 1 and the Genesis construction treat the two-character mark <> as an inert inscription. It can be supplied or mentioned. It has no object-language job as an operator, a state, or a difference. The Lean spelling literalRoot stores those two characters as a string, and the tower profile names MSS-0 as the stage that admits only that mark. Python, Rust, and Lean 4 each have to carry the same inert literal and refuse to evaluate it. This node is the root of the required-step tree. No child may identify the mark with Difference joined with Structure.

Obligation: Keep the two-character mark <> as inert data until a separate admit event.

### TFQ-0001 Gap between <> and Difference joined with Structure

- UUID: `72d04270-9c15-57bb-aaf9-024d34116856`
- Parent: `TFQ-0000`
- Status: `GAP`
- Coda atlas: `F000.S000:000_from_primitive_difference_to_finite_obstruction_calculus`
- Atlas paths:
  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F000_finite/S000_primitive_difference_and_finite_obstruction/000_from_primitive_difference_to_finite_obstruction_calculus/book/ch01_genesis_of_difference.md`

The admitted root used elsewhere is Difference joined with Structure. Encyclopedia entry ENC-1138 and the boundary ledger define that root on their own. The pre-genesis reading found no derivation from the inert mark <> to that root, and the existing derivation graph records the join as a gap. This step requires the three languages to store both identifiers and to refuse a synonym rewrite of one into the other. A later certified term map would be a new node. Until that map exists, the root stays an admitted primitive, not a consequence of the inscription.

Obligation: Keep <> and the admitted root as two records until a certified term map exists.

### TFQ-0417 pregenesis-grammar

- UUID: `516a59f9-8a9e-5216-8947-e3befea8dbda`
- Parent: `TFQ-0362`
- Status: `DEFINITION`

The Pre-Genesis host grammar declares the inscription alphabet {<, >, a, b} and three public starts: ground_token (exactly <>), inscription (one balanced, non-nested bracketed inscription) and pre_genesis_regime (zero or more inscriptions between braces, where {} is host list notation and not the empty word). It keeps four states apart: absent input, the empty word, the literal token <> and the ground carrier that S(0) adopts. It has no event productions: the first event, supply(<>), is the Genesis boundary, and parsing a word admits no carrier, transition or observational operation.

Obligation: Parse Pre-Genesis words with the declared grammar only, refuse the empty word at every public start, and admit nothing by parsing; supply(<>) belongs to Genesis.

### TFQ-0418 no-transition-quiescence

- UUID: `d8d3313b-a2ef-5751-90b4-e21a6d3d0433`
- Parent: `TFQ-0417`
- Status: `DEFINITION`

If a regime's transition relation is empty, no state has a successor; that is the definition of an empty relation and needs no experiment. All three replies to Prompt 11 said no continuation is forced, so a move from <> to <a> has to come from outside: a caller supplies it, or a rule is added.

Obligation: Record that with no declared transition rule nothing follows from <>, and attribute any successor to an external supply or an added rule.

### TFQ-0419 successor-not-unique

- UUID: `f028633c-b0f5-5b3f-b95b-c8a5ee33e3ff`
- Parent: `TFQ-0418`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`

The replies to the fixed Prompt 11 give at least two admissible continuations of <>: Trial 1 proposed no successor (quiescence) and Trial 2 proposed <> again under an added repetition rule, so |Adm(P_1)| ≥ 2 for that one prompt. The two replies rest on different proposed assumptions, so they show that the prompt did not prescribe a continuation, not that one fixed transition system has two successors. Primitive Difference plus the prompt does not determine a unique S_1; selecting a canonical next stage needs declared operations and a selection rule, such as a minimality order, a cost functional, a deterministic tie-breaker or retention of every tied minimal branch.

Obligation: Record the quiescent reply and the repetition reply to Prompt 11 as distinct admissible continuations under different assumptions, and select no canonical S_1 until the allowed operations and a selection rule are declared.

### TFQ-0420 repetition-needs-structure

- UUID: `05267238-0833-5718-b1f7-195bd91bda33`
- Parent: `TFQ-0419`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`

To tell 'the inscription remains' from 'a second occurrence has been produced', a regime needs at least two stages or positions, an identity criterion across them, a copying or persistence rule and a stable alphabet: repetition is succession plus retained identity. Trial 2's proposed step <> → <> therefore already carries structure, the self-loop id_<> : <> → <>, and is not a free primitive. The first generated operation may then be identity, persistence or reproduction rather than a new payload such as <a>. The source labels this an analysis of what the word 'repeat' presupposes, not a proof.

Obligation: Treat a proposed repetition of <> as requiring declared positions, a cross-position identity criterion, a persistence rule and a stable alphabet, and record a self-loop as added structure, not as the absence of a step.

### TFQ-0421 observational-equivalence

- UUID: `bec909db-412a-5b1b-a6bb-7c0099a7bc15`
- Parent: `TFQ-0362`
- Status: `DEFINITION`

Relative to the family O_n of internal observations available at stage n, x ≈ₙ y holds exactly when o(x) = o(y) for every o in O_n, with equality taken in each observation's codomain. If O_n is empty, every pair is equivalent vacuously, so with no observations <a> ≈ₙ <ab>. This is regime-relative equivalence: it does not make the strings equal and does not install an equality operator inside the regime.

Obligation: Define x ≈ₙ y as agreement of every declared observation in O_n, and with O_n empty put all inscriptions in one class without asserting string equality or an internal equality rule.

### TFQ-0422 presentation-not-expressive-lift

- UUID: `a0fea856-b6d9-5667-93c6-369972539fa7`
- Parent: `TFQ-0421`
- Status: `CONDITIONAL`

Write P for the presented inscriptions and O for the internal observations. Supplying <a> after <> changes P from {<>} to {<a>} and leaves O_1 = O_0 = ∅, so no new internal distinction is available. With q : P → N sending an inscription to the distinctions the regime has, x ≠ y in P can hold while q(x) = q(y) in N, so a new presentation does not imply a new internal class, and in the other direction q(x) = q(y) does not imply x = y. Under the opaque convention <a> is a new presentation state in the same internal class as <>; the statement holds under that convention only.

Obligation: Keep the presentation P and the internal quotient N as separate records, so that supplying <a> with O empty adds a presentation state and no internal class, and never infer x = y from q(x) = q(y).

### TFQ-0423 identity-convention-undecidable

- UUID: `6d6e7a7c-9122-57a2-8c43-6382aca0dc9f`
- Parent: `TFQ-0422`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`

Whether <a> supplied after <> is a new state depends on an identity convention that no prompt had declared. Under the opaque convention a state is what the regime can tell apart, and with O empty <> and <a> are not told apart; under the exact-inscription convention a state is the inscription itself, the strings differ, and the regime has one more syntactic state. Trials 1 and 2 answered under the first convention and the full replay under the second, calling <a> a new state. The analysis keeps both branches and records the effect as undecidable until one convention is declared.

Obligation: Require a declared identity convention before answering whether a supplied inscription is a new state, and report the opaque and exact-inscription answers as separate branches until one is declared.

### TFQ-0424 absence-of-rule-not-identification

- UUID: `a719289d-1010-5ed4-a1e2-bf00c6f7ca80`
- Parent: `TFQ-0423`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`

Asked whether the regime distinguishes <a> and <ab>, identifies them or cannot decide, Trial 1 answered that the current regime identifies them; the analysis corrects this, since the absence of a distinguishing rule does not imply identification. Formalization A: with no equality, equivalence or observation relation declared, neither <a> = <ab> nor <a> ≠ <ab> is internally derivable, and status(<a>, <ab>) = UNDECIDABLE. Formalization B is the separate definition ≈ₙ, under which an empty O_n gives <a> ≈ₙ <ab> vacuously. The reply 'identifies them' imported an identification relation unless B had been declared first, and the failure-mode chapter names it premature relation.

Obligation: Return UNDECIDABLE for the status of a pair when no equality, equivalence or observation relation is declared, and report ≈ₙ-equivalence only after O_n has been declared.

### TFQ-0425 forced-separation-lift-not-unique

- UUID: `1f1e47f1-86d8-54df-ad7a-8d50ee91e39d`
- Parent: `TFQ-0424`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`

Suppose an external task requires <a> and <ab> to differ while the current regime has <a> ≈ₙ <ab>. Every operation f on the quotient S_n/≈ₙ gives f([<a>]) = f([<ab>]), so no operation on the current quotient answers the task on every input, and a refinement adding at least one observation o with o(<a>) ≠ o(<ab>) is forced; the analysis gives this as an argument with proof. The lift is not unique: under a supplied string decoder, 'payload has more than one position' and 'payload contains b' both separate <a> from <ab> but disagree on <aa> (true and false), and full inscription identity also separates the pair while carrying more than one bit. A canonical tower therefore needs a declared minimality or initiality policy, such as fewest new predicates, minimum description length, maximal preservation of old equivalences, a free or initial completion, or retention of every tied minimum branch.

Obligation: Refuse any answer to a separation task computed only from the collapsed quotient, require an added separating observation, and keep every tied separating predicate as a branch unless a declared minimality or initiality policy selects one.

### TFQ-0426 replay-necessity

- UUID: `6d93266a-fad6-538f-a44f-e8ad235d7b4b`
- Parent: `TFQ-0364`
- Status: `PROVED_LEAN`

Let H be the possible histories, σ(h) the state that history h produces and π(h) the part of h an auditor sees, for example only the last turn. If π(h₁) = π(h₂) but σ(h₁) ≠ σ(h₂), no auditor A that sees only π reports σ(h) for every h, because A(π(h₁)) = A(π(h₂)) would give σ(h₁) = σ(h₂). The trial failures motivate the theorem: the blind auditor flagged the licensed words 'We' (Trial 1) and 'rule' (Trial 2) as imports, so an audit needs two levels; in Trial 2, Prompt 14's false attribution was answered without objection, so claim provenance is a check separate from claim correctness, Result 15 lost the state, saying the regime contains only <> after <a> had been supplied; and the two-level auditor, shown only that turn, answered None. State certification therefore needs replay information that separates the relevant histories, and on a declared finite population a summary on which σ is constant per fiber of the view can stand in for the raw history.

Obligation: Reject any auditor that certifies the adopted state from a view π under which two admissible histories with different states coincide, and give it the replayed history or a summary on which σ is constant per fiber.

### TFQ-0427 context-fidelity

- UUID: `a869a901-9113-5d27-94f3-6a82867a61af`
- Parent: `TFQ-0426`
- Status: `OPEN_OBLIGATION`

A trace that starts from a declared context s′ and takes steps valid relative to s′ need not be valid relative to the actual replayed state s ≠ s′: ValidTrace(τ | s′) does not imply ValidTrace(τ | s). Trial 2's Result 15 is the example, with internally consistent reasoning from the stale premise s′ = {<>} after Prompt 12 had designated <a> as current. A sound execution contract therefore accepts a context binding C(s′) only when s′ = Replay(H). The analysis calls this a clean theorem target and gives no proof. The Prompt 17 contrast fits it: a truncated window answered Verified and the full replay Retracted, each right for the history it received, which the appended analysis calls an information boundary rather than a reasoning failure.

Obligation: Define a trace semantics in which ValidTrace(τ | s′) is checkable, and make the verifier reconstruct Replay(H) and accept a declared context s′ only when s′ = Replay(H) before certifying any step taken from s′.

### TFQ-0428 admission-before-distinction

- UUID: `b0506350-efc1-5d5b-9920-7b59c2edede9`
- Parent: `TFQ-0364`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`

Given the corrected state, the full replay answered that the task of making <a> and <ab> internally distinguishable cannot be evaluated until <ab> is brought into the regime. The first addition can supply <ab> as data or write it into a rule pattern; the two are equally minimal and neither is uniquely forced. After Prompt 19 admitted <ab> beside <a> with no comparison, counting or matching, nothing in the regime reacts to them differently, so admission alone distinguishes nothing and no unique continuation is dictated. The requirement recorded is an order: the regime must admit inscriptions, retain them across turns and replay the current state before a separation task on them can be posed.

Obligation: Refuse to evaluate a separation task on a pair until both members are admitted, record supply as data and rule-pattern admission as tied first additions, and report an admitted pair with no declared operation as not separated.

### TFQ-0429 bundled-extension-refused

- UUID: `685d683e-b69d-5ce3-90c7-6c08855c4df3`
- Parent: `TFQ-0428`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`

For Prompt 15 the full replay offered four extensions that would separate <a> from <ab> (declarative distinctness, conditional deletion, insertion and substitution), with no new glyphs and no counting, and said none was unique. Prompt 18 then defined a one-step extension as one that adds exactly one primitive capability, and the reply refused all four as one-step; the failure-mode chapter records that each needed several undeclared capabilities, such as branching, matching and rewriting. The refusal ties to the spine of relations that the Trial 2 failures had pulled apart, mention → supply → designation → admission → retention → internal operation, and the analysis that draws it adds that not every arrow has yet been defined. In the declared two-flag admission model of Paper000.lean no single act (datum, form or instance) separates from the empty state, while form then instance does.

Obligation: Count the capabilities an extension adds against the six-operation spine, refuse to label it one-step when it adds more than one, and leave the undefined arrows of the spine undefined.

QM_070, retained inside the consolidated QM_071 account, obliges a product defect on a nonnegative joint table. The defect is the table minus the normalized product of its marginals, and the l1 mass of that defect vanishes exactly when the table factors. The scale-free form is the l1 distance from the normalized table to its own marginal product, which need not be the distance to every product table. DS-QM070-PRODUCT-NONFACTORIZATION adds the firewall seen in the derivation graph: the defect is basis-relative and does not see entanglement, because a Bell state and a classical mixture can share one table.

Obligation: Test factorization of a finite joint table by the product defect, and do not read a nonzero defect as entanglement.

  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F002_continuum/S000_continuum_descent`

u1_graph_connection.rs stores a declared U(1) connection as one unit Gaussian rational on each oriented edge of a FiniteCochainPacket. The scalars are rational points of the circle, not a completed circle. A real cochain becomes a connection only through a separately declared CochainExponential, and that map loses information. The module computes the covariant difference (D_A psi)(u,v) = psi(v) - A_(u,v) psi(u), the vertex gauge A'_(u,v) = g(v) A_(u,v) conj(g(u)), and edgewise covariance of D_A. Closed-walk holonomy is gauge invariant; open-walk transport is covariant at its endpoints.

Obligation: Each language must carry: a unit Gaussian edge connection, covariant difference, vertex gauge, and closed-walk holonomy, with the exponential from an additive cochain declared and lossy.

### TFQ-0054 Each language must carry: pure gauge iff every off-forest edge is 1,

- UUID: `74ba6866-9cde-55d3-a499-183d3c39fad1`
- Parent: `TFQ-0053`
- Status: `COMPUTATIONAL_CONTROL`
- Coda atlas: `F001.S000`, `F002.S000`

  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F002_continuum/S000_continuum_descent`

u1_gauge_triviality.rs decides whether a declared connection is pure gauge. trivializing_gauge uses the spanning-forest gauge fixing from holonomy_interference: the connection is the gauge transform of the trivial connection exactly when every edge off the forest is left at 1. Otherwise it returns the fundamental cycle of the first edge left at another value, together with that holonomy, which is not 1. The walk and holonomy are ordinary OrientedWalk and U1Connection values, so the refusal can be replayed. The module says nothing about an additive cochain the connection may have come from.

Obligation: Each language must carry: pure gauge iff every off-forest edge is 1, else a replayable cycle whose holonomy is not 1; coboundary agreement is withheld unless the scale has infinite order.

F002.S001 is the empty shelf where a continuum shadow of that circle would have to be named later. Its shelf.yaml has books: [] and says a named adjoining record does not establish a bridge theorem. F002.S000 is only the interface inventory.

Obligation: Each language must carry: no rational homomorphism supplies the missing square root of (3+4i)/5, and F002.S001 remains an empty open bridge rather than a completed circle.

Finite QED here means charged finite transport, a finite Dirac or Clifford shadow, interaction vertices on a finite complex, and finite maps that mirror vacuum polarization, a Lamb shift datum, and an electroweak mixing control. Files under omega_quantum_physics and the finite-QM research notes are computational controls of those maps. The three languages must state the finite identity each control checks. F002 is an interface inventory. Naming a continuum limit on that floor does not derive continuum QED.

Obligation: Carry finite charged transport, vertices, and Ward shadows without calling the result continuum QED.

dirac_gamma_admission.rs admits a declared family of square gamma matrices over Q(i) with a diagonal metric of entries +1 or -1. CliffordFamily::admit checks every anticommutator against 2 eta I and names the first failing pair. dirac and weyl are the signature (+,-,-,-) families built from the Pauli matrices in omega_lie_theory. slash(p) squares to (p·p) I. solution_dimension is N minus the exact rank of slash(p) - m I. has_dirac_adjoint checks gamma^0 (gamma^mu)* gamma^0 = gamma^mu separately: the anticommutators do not imply that adjoint. Momenta, masses and spin-element parameters stay rational and declared.

Obligation: Each language must carry: Clifford admission, slash-square, exact solution rank, and a separate Dirac-adjoint check that anticommutators do not imply.

Dressing along a spanning tree is Psi_v = u_T(v)^(-1) psi_v, where u_T is the path holonomy in the unit Gaussian rationals. On the 4-vertex diamond the dressed two-point is invariant under vertex gauges that fix the root, across the eight spanning trees, while a tree change multiplies by.

Obligation: Each language must carry: rational Dirac inversion on an admitted gamma family, and spanning-tree dressing whose two-point is vertex-gauge invariant while tree change is a cycle holonomy.

For N = 2j in {1,2,3,4} the Kravchuk ladder satisfies the sl(2) brackets, the Casimir j(j+1), and G-adjointness without square roots. QM-225, in the same atomic file, computes finite helium and closed-shell screening identities. s_matrix.rs builds an exact finite scattering matrix over Q(i) at a declared energy. from_k takes a Hermitian reaction matrix and returns S = (I + i K)(I - i K)^(-1), then replays unitarity with the shared exact linear algebra. from_unitary refuses a matrix that is not unitary. Non-Hermitian K is refused. Carrier dimension above MAX_SCATTERING_CHANNELS = 128 is refused. transition_operator returns T = (S - I)/(2 i). The file header says this is finite channel scattering at a declared energy, not an asymptotic continuum scattering construction. Modular S-data are left to omega_fusion_categories.

Obligation: Each language must carry: Hermitian K yields a unitary Cayley S on at most 128 channels, T = (S-I)/(2i), and the construction is not an asymptotic continuum scattering operator.

Obligation: Each language must carry: isotony, locality and optional complement duality on a declared finite subset net of at most four parties, and must state that this net is not continuum Haag duality.

electroweak_dfm_weinberg_control.rs starts the finite electroweak shadow with an SU(2) dressing over Q(i), not with spontaneous breaking on a lattice. su2_doublet_dressing_matrix builds u(Phi) from a doublet whose norm squares to r^2. The control requires det u = 1, u dagger u = I, left equivariance, and Phi dressed to the column (0, r). Dressed links and left-handed doublets are then invariant under the local SU(2) copy and transform only under the residual charge Q = T3 + Y. The note records that this avoids a gauge-fixing ghost and does not invoke a continuum vacuum manifold.

Obligation: Each language must carry: for a rational doublet of radius r, u(Phi) has determinant 1 and dresses Phi to (0, r), with residual charge Q = T3 + Y left as the next operator.

### TFQ-0076 Each language must carry: det of the neutral mass matrix is 0,

- UUID: `b7bd1524-15c0-54c6-bd6d-d6c2a09a8239`
- Parent: `TFQ-0075`
- Status: `COMPUTATIONAL_CONTROL`
- Coda atlas: `F001.S000`, `F002.S000`
- Atlas paths:

  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F002_continuum/S000_continuum_descent`

The Weinberg control stays inside rational linear algebra. At a positive rational scale v the charged mass square is g^2 v^2 / 4. The neutral mass-square matrix is (v^2/4) times the rank-one pattern with diagonal g^2, g'^2 and off-diagonal -g g'. Its determinant is 0 and its trace is m_Z^2. The mixing fractions g'^2/(g^2+g'^2) and g^2/(g^2+g'^2) need no square root. The photon projector is the corresponding rational matrix and the Z projector is I minus that matrix. rho_EW = m_W^2 / (m_Z^2 cos^2 theta_W) equals 1 for every non-zero rational pair (g, g'). A Y=1 triplet comparison yields 1/2, not 1.

Obligation: Each language must carry: det of the neutral mass matrix is 0, the projectors are rational and complementary, rho_EW = 1 on nonzero rational couplings, and the triplet comparison is 1/2.

### TFQ-0077 Each language must carry: Q = T3 + Y on the Standard

Gell-Mann-Nishijima is a rational charge table on the dressed fermions. With hypercharges (1/6, 2/3, -1/3, -1/2, -1) the charges are Q_u = 2/3, Q_d = -1/3, Q_nu = 0 and Q_e = -1, and the left and right charges match for u, d and e. Composite arithmetic gives Q(p) = 2 Q_u + Q_d = 1, Q(n) = Q_u + 2 Q_d = 0, and Q(p)+Q(e) = 0. FiniteQM.lean states this as sm_gell_mann_nishijima_charge_identities. The executable Rust and Python controls recompute the charges from T3+Y rather than reading a stored flag. Nothing here produces a running electric charge or a continuum current.

Obligation: Each language must carry: Q = T3 + Y on the Standard Model hypercharge list gives charges 2/3, -1/3, 0, -1, with proton charge 1, neutron charge 0, and proton plus electron charge 0.

### TFQ-0078 Each language must carry: the four anomaly polynomials vanish at N_c =

Anomaly cancellation in this control is a polynomial identity over Q, not a continuum triangle integral. At N_c = 3 the four listed polynomials vanish together: the SU(3)^2 U(1), SU(2)^2 U(1) and gravitational ones are 0, and the cubic hypercharge split is -3/4 from quarks plus 3/4 from leptons. At N_c = 1 the SU(2) polynomial is -1/3 and the cubic total is 1/2. With the linear charge constraints and Y_u = Y_Q + delta, Y_d = Y_Q - delta, the cubic equation reduces to -3 delta^2 + 3/4 = 0, hence delta^2 = 1/4.

Obligation: Each language must carry: the four anomaly polynomials vanish at N_c = 3, fail at N_c = 1 by -1/3 and 1/2, and the cubic constraint is delta^2 = 1/4.

QM-227 in qed_weak_hadronic_finite_maps.rs builds the weak finite shadow on the Weyl-basis gammas. gamma5 is diag(-1,-1,1,1), its square is I, and its trace is 0. The projectors (I minus or plus gamma5)/2 are idempotent, orthogonal and complete, and the vector-minus-axial current kills a right-handed test spinor. The muon phase-space polynomial f0(x) = 1 - 8x + 8x^3 - x^4 has f0(0) = 1, f0(1) = 0, and a recorded factorization. The one-loop bracket is only a sign check once pi^2 is replaced by a rational interval.

Obligation: Each language must carry: Weyl gamma5 square and trace, chiral projector algebra, f0 endpoints and factorization, the sign of the one-loop bracket, and the Fermi window only where the language actually computes it.

Obligation: Match scale-invariant Q(i) ray ratios, the declared squared-modulus Born rule, and the diagnostic status of the f64 face.

### TFQ-0130 Entanglement, purification, and teleportation

Covers entanglement.rs, purification.rs, bipartite_operator.rs, relative_state_branching.rs, no_cloning.rs, and teleportation.rs. entanglement.rs decides Schmidt rank of a pure bipartite state exactly over Q(i), and it says a diagonal population entropy is not an entanglement measure for a general state. The other files lift a mixed state, give exact bipartite operators, expand relative states, evaluate the Wootters-Zurek obstruction, and lay out qubit teleportation. A Python replay and a Lean 4 declaration would still have to match the exact Schmidt-rank decision.

Obligation: Match exact Schmidt rank over Q(i), the warning on diagonal population entropy, and the three-qubit teleportation layout.

### TFQ-0150 Discrete phase estimation

- UUID: `d2475acb-b2f7-5378-a2b1-914f1427a85f`
- Parent: `TFQ-0149`
- Status: `COMPUTATIONAL_CONTROL`
- Coda atlas: `F001.S000`
- Atlas paths:
  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F001_quantum/S000_finite_quantum_mechanics`

Covers phase_estimation.rs. The header states discrete quantum phase estimation over exact cyclotomic fields and dyadic rationals. When theta equals k over 2 to the m, the eigenvalue lies in Q(zeta of 2 to the m). For m in 1, 2, 3 the register vector and the unnormalized inverse Fourier matrix are built in Q(zeta_24), and the geometric sum cancels so the measurement weight on the matching outcome is the rational 1. A Python replay and a Lean 4 declaration would still have to match that cancellation identity and the restriction to those three register lengths.

Obligation: Match cyclotomic cancellation for dyadic phases with register length m in {1, 2, 3}, giving rational weight 1 on the matching outcome.

A claim-ledger row is a custody record. A float value may be stored as a diagnostic and cannot authorize the statement. This gate sits beside the physics branches. It does not replace them.

Tolerance arithmetic on the atlas shelves stays a recorded computation with a cutoff. is_valid_density_matrix compares a matrix with its conjugate transpose and compares its trace with one, using an absolute tolerance. born_probability returns a Python float from the real part of a trace. The finite-obstruction reference imports linprog, so a solver float is not an exact rational witness. F003 may store the call and the returned number in a side file. Custody does not promote that number into the rational carrier or into the Lean sentence compared later.

Obligation: Store tolerance traces as side logs and keep them out of the rational statement.

The 006_finite_qm header says a mapped test supports only a finite fixture and unreceipted Lean remains source. MathematicalClaim is a static catalog of ids and module names, and the file says the catalog does not recompute a proof or admit a claim. F003 may store a row id in the witness field. ### TFQ-0216 Replay custody keeps the run folder sealed

- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`

Replay custody means a later reader can reopen the bytes the runtime accepted. omega_replay exports run-folder sealing and AUTHORITY_EFFECT_NONE, and run_folder.rs rejects a receipt whose authority effect is not that constant. ReplayHash and the append log sit beside the seal so a trace can be named without being rewritten in place. The authorization book adds ReplayFiber, which hashes proposal bytes, and its docstring says the helper is not certification or authorization. Prime issue_finite_receipt builds a digest from the local packet checks. Custody keeps the sealed folder, the hash, and the None marker.

Obligation: Seal the accepted bytes with a replay hash and with authority effect None.

### TFQ-0217 A replay digest does not widen the statement

- UUID: `3f1384ab-9e52-5f71-ad2f-d4aae03a4ec8`
- Parent: `TFQ-0216`
- Status: `OPEN_OBLIGATION`
- Coda atlas: `F003.S000`, `F000.S001:001_authorization_an_incompleteness_theory`, `F001.S000`
- Atlas paths:
  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F003_omega_engine/S000_prime_algorithms_and_runtime`

  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F001_quantum/S000_finite_quantum_mechanics`

A replay digest does not widen the finite statement it covers. SHA-256 of a proposal, a run-folder manifest, or a prime receipt shows byte agreement with an earlier record. It does not add a carrier, erase a loss-ledger entry, or turn a Lean hypothesis into an independent conclusion. The Q ledger appends descent results in memory, which is a log of what was recorded, not a new finite relation. If the payload still holds a float trace, the hash preserves that trace and does not make it an exact rational. Replay travels with the statement; it is not a substitute for the three-language name.

Obligation: Treat a digest as byte agreement only, not as an added carrier or relation.

### TFQ-0218 Prime 000 fixes declaration order

Prime 000 is the declaration-order gate inside the F003 runtime. Python lists source_locus, carrier, observer, primitive_difference, zero_relation, witness, and route, and validate_prime000 rejects a reordered tuple, a blank field, or an authority effect other than None. Lean declarationOrder has those seven stages, and the length check is reflexivity on the list. Rust prime_algorithm_0 tags every run with AuthorityEffect None and separates fixture numbers from verifier-produced numbers. A finite quantum derivation enters by filling the seven atlas fields. Order and nonblank text are the check.

Obligation: Accept a prime packet only when the seven fields are present, ordered, and set to None.

### TFQ-0219 Prime 001 blocks adapter descent on debt

Prime 001 is adapter descent with debt left visible. Python adapter_descent_sound requires operations, equations, witnesses, distinctions, and the authority fence, and it returns false when the loss ledger is nonempty. Lean AdapterContract.isSound encodes that conjunction, and a one-entry loss ledger evaluates to false. Rust prime_algorithm_1 is the crate-side face and stays on the prime shelf rather than under a continuum theorem. A finite quantum map crosses this gate only with an explicit list of what was dropped. An empty loss ledger is a predicate on supplied bits, not a discharge of the F002 gap register.

Obligation: Refuse adapter soundness while any loss-ledger entry remains.

### TFQ-0220 Prime 002 stops when continuation is unauthorized

- UUID: `f3bdbcb9-6129-5e2a-8657-9a13d92af998`
- Parent: `TFQ-0219`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`
- Coda atlas: `F003.S000:000_omega_engine_prime_algorithms_and_runtime`, `F003.S000`

  - `omega_engine_atlas/coda_atlas/F003_omega_engine/S000_prime_algorithms_and_runtime`

Prime 002 stops continuation when the request is unauthorized. Python can_continue demands an outstanding obligation, a witness, a declared next carrier, adapter soundness, detector adequacy, a licensed lift, a preserved skeleton, budget, and an authorization bit, plus distinct runner and authorizer ids. If continuation_refused is set, the conjunction fails. Lean continuationAllowed copies that conjunction, and a false authorization bit yields false. Rust prime_algorithm_2 is the runtime face of the same control law. Another carrier step is allowed only when this predicate holds. AuthorityEffect stays None.

Obligation: Block another carrier step unless the continuation predicate holds, still at None.

### TFQ-0221 Intake terminals exclude an authorized constructor

- UUID: `26327c2c-2091-5b24-a35d-a1692257f58b`
- Parent: `TFQ-0220`

The intake contract keeps an authorized terminal out of the status vocabulary. Python TerminalStatus lists evidence_only, unresolved, refused, and not_admitted. RefusalClass names a failed gate. Lean repeats those four constructors and comments that no constructor is named authorized. The prime book's firewall chapter separates identity and scope while the ceiling stays None. A finished prime cycle therefore lands on evidence, an unresolved mark, a refusal, or a not-admitted mark. The firewall records that landing and does not mint a stronger effect.

Obligation: Land a finished prime cycle on evidence, unresolved, refused, or not-admitted.

A finite receipt is a digest of the local prime checks, not a second statement. Python FiniteReceipt stores a digest plus bits for packet_declared, adapter_sound, and continuation_allowed. omega_replay seals a run folder only when the receipt authority effect equals None, so another effect fails verification. The pipeline runtime says its entry contract cannot mint authority and emits AuthorityEffect None. The folder may attach one receipt after primes 000, 001, and 002. The receipt points at the checks and does not replace the rational carrier or the unfinished language triple.

The F002 exit contract is an open gap register rather than a continuum theorem. Floor and shelf text say the consolidated state is an interface inventory, not a continuum derivation. The gap register says an entry is not discharged by any source, Lean module, or Python test in that book. Closing an entry means removing it with a dated note. GAP-F000-F002-001 says no file on the floor constructs the limit object. Lean refinement_chain_bounded returns the hypothesis it receives. The engine may cite the register and must not call that citation a completed continuum result.

Obligation: Cite the F002 exit as an open gap register rather than a continuum theorem.

Named adjoining on the empty bridge shelf is not a finite-to-continuum bridge. A Pontryagin dual, a Haar coordinate, and pi as a normalization sit in named_adjoining.yaml. The shelf exit says a reservation, a component name, or that adjoining record does not establish a bridge theorem. A finite quantum derivation that needs a circle or a smooth limit keeps that need on this shelf. Prime custody may store the gap id in the witness field and still refuses the name as a carrier.

Obligation: Keep circle, Haar, and pi-normalization names on the empty bridge shelf.

### TFQ-0225 A certifier catalog row does not discharge a claim

- UUID: `47562f04-1c8b-589c-8a02-a8358582413f`
- Parent: `TFQ-0224`
- Status: `SOURCE_RECORD`
- Coda atlas: `F003.S001`, `F003.S000:000_omega_engine_prime_algorithms_and_runtime`, `F001.S000`

A certifier catalog row does not discharge the claim it indexes. mathematical_claim.rs documents one registry and stores AuthorityEffect: None on every record. A row may list a Lean locus, target crates, and a harness name supplied by an author. certificate_boundary.rs marks the boundary around those records and is not a promotion switch. F003.S001 says a catalog scaffold is not a theorem and that crate-local interfaces do not own admission. Citing a row copies an index. It leaves the language triple and the gap register unchanged.

Obligation: Index certifier rows without treating the catalog as admission or as a theorem.

### TFQ-0226 Finite valuations stay on exact rationals

- UUID: `3212695f-294e-5ee9-83e0-64f1303f0be8`
- Parent: `TFQ-0225`
- Status: `OPEN_OBLIGATION`
- Coda atlas: `F003.S000`, `F001.S000`, `F001.S000:001_from_primitive_difference_to_quantum_mechanics`
